# Parity port: deviation register

One row per deliberate difference from v1: the flag id, what differs, why, who approved it and the test that pins the new behaviour. Nothing deviates without a row (spec `2026-10-17-parity-port-design.md`, §5.6). The two rules: "numbers never deviate without OJ; copy never deviates without Dr. Sharma".

- **Ids.** An audit flag keeps its `F` number from §4 of `PORT-AUDIT.md`, in `appuscholar-blueprint/` (a sibling folder outside this repo): `F5`. A letter suffix (`F23a`) marks a sub-finding of an audit flag that the plan introduces. A new row is `D-W<wave>-<n>`, with `n` counting up within the wave. Faults found on a live walk are named by the walk (`W1` to `W29` for the 2026-10-20 stage 2 walk, `appuscholar-blueprint/notes/2026-10-20-stage2-live-walk.md`) and are never ids; a fix for one takes the next `D-S2-<n>`. Wave B rows start at D-S2-38 (plan `2026-10-21-wave-b-redesign.md`); D-S2-37 is the W30 capture-year patch.
- **Statuses.**
    - `proposed`: awaits OJ for numbers, Dr. Sharma for copy.
    - `approved`: accepted; the approver and date are in the row.
    - `rejected`: declined; no change is made.
    - `noted`: asks for no decision — it changes no output, or the difference was decided in an earlier phase and is recorded here so it is not rediscovered.
- **Adding a row.** Every task that deviates appends a row at the bottom as `proposed` or `noted`, with all eight cells filled (`—` where there is nothing yet) and no `|` inside a cell (write "or", or a comma). A golden mismatch is a port bug or a row, never a hand-edited fixture.
- **Lines and dates.** v1 line numbers are those of `index.html` build 2026.10.13a (13,699 lines). The date in "approver and date" is the day the approval was recorded here (YYYY-MM-DD).

| D-W1-12 | `apToks` 9856, as used by `apPick` 9992 → `analyse.ts` (`apToks`, `apPick`) | `apToks` lower-cases the text, splits it on every run of characters that are not a letter or a number (`[^\p{L}\p{N}]+`, with the `u` flag) and keeps the pieces of more than 3 characters that are not in `AP_STOP`. A Devanagari vowel sign, like the virama, is a combining mark and not a letter, so it ends a piece: an ordinary Hindi word falls into pieces of one to three letters, which the length test drops (a run of four or more consonant letters with no sign between them would survive). The fixture's Hindi question gives no token. `apPick` (9991–9993) scores the pieces of a paper longer than 1.5 times the budget by the question's tokens, so it cannot score a Hindi question: it keeps the opening pieces in order, because only the first piece scores (2, 9993) | ported as is | verbatim port (spec §5.1); recorded for Dr. Sharma, a fix is a copy or number change | proposed | OJ | Task 11 `analyse.test.ts`, the `apToks` cases (`नींद और परीक्षा की चिंता को कम करना` gives `[]`) |

Every Devanagari letter is taken out, so every all-Devanagari title, like any title with no ASCII letter, digit or underscore, has the empty key and, of several different Hindi titles, only the first survives; a title that also holds Latin letters or digits keeps those as its key | ported as is | verbatim port (spec §5.1); recorded for Dr. Sharma, a fix is a copy or number change | proposed | OJ | Task 12 `research.test.ts`, `rsDedupe` (inputs #3 and #9: two different Hindi titles, the second dropped) |

## Carried to later waves

Findings of Wave 0–1 that belong to a later wave's plan, so they are not rediscovered. Each is deleted from here once that plan carries it.

- **(b) Four innerText reads F26 does not inventory, for the Wave 3 plan.** v1 reads `innerText` of an attached element at 3558 (the editor's word count, `editor.innerText`), 6814 (a fix applied to the selection, `ed.innerText.indexOf(fix)`), 12473 (`#statsSentence`) and 13527 (`#pwSentence`, copied to the clipboard). An audit gap noted by Task 2 (ledger line 62); the Wave 3 plan rules on each when it ports the screens that read them.
